Correlator
\boldsymbol{\langle\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle}

. The correlator

(4.6)

\displaystyle{\hyperlink{eqref:TQHHHDef}{\mathbb{T}_{2}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptI}}}}

4.2.2
Free theory

With the definitions in (
4.4
) and (
4.5
) we can compute the free theory four-point function by taking Wick contraction and performing the traces over the various indices. The result can be written as follows

\langle\mathcal{O}_{02}(x_{1},{\mathbf{S}}_{1})\mathcal{O}_{2}(x_{2},y_{2})\mathcal{O}_{2}(x_{3},y_{3})\mathcal{O}_{2}(x_{4},y_{4})\rangle={\hyperlink{eqref:kinPref}{\mathcal{K}_{4222}}}\,\sum_{k=1}^{3}g_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})\,{\hyperlink{eqref:TQHHHDef}{\mathbb{T}_{k}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptI}}}}\,,

(4.7)

We will not give the explicit expression of the functions
g_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})
here. However, in table
2
we provide a summary of the operators exchanged in each tensor structure. Let us emphasize that the operators that we show are conformal primaries, not superconformal primaries. To know the expansion in the latter one would have to use the superconformal blocks which are not yet known. Note however that, unlike the following cases, no operators of twist higher than four are exchanged. The reason for this is that the entire correlation function is actually protected. Thus there are no long operators at threshold that are expected to gain anomalous dimensions when the coupling is turned on. A detailed proof of the protected nature of this correlator is given in section
7
and it follows from the study of the OPE limit of the
\mathcal{O}_{2}
five-point function.

24

As a consistency check, here and in all the following examples, we have computed
\mathfrak{f}(z)
by applying the chiral algebra map to the free-theory result in

).

\frac{\chi\big[\langle\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle\big]}{{\hyperlink{eqref:kinPrefz}{\mathfrak{K}_{4222}}}}\equiv{\mathfrak{f}}_{\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}}(z)=-\frac{\sqrt{N^{2}-4}}{2\sqrt{3}(N^{2}-1)}\left(z^{2}+\frac{z}{z-1}\right)\,.

(4.8)

The contribution of
{\mathfrak{f}}
to the full correlator can be written as follows — we omit the subscript for brevity

\frac{\langle\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle}{{\hyperlink{eqref:kinPref}{\mathcal{K}_{4222}}}}\big|_{{\mathfrak{f}}}={\hyperlink{eqref:TQHHHDef}{\mathbb{T}_{1}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptI}}}}\,\frac{2z{\bar{z}}\,\bigl(z\hskip 1.0pt{\mathfrak{f}}({\bar{z}})-{\bar{z}}\hskip 1.0pt{\mathfrak{f}}(z)\bigr)}{z-{\bar{z}}}+{\hyperlink{eqref:TQHHHDef}{\mathbb{T}_{3}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptI}}}}\,\frac{4\,\bigl(z^{2}({\bar{z}}-2)\hskip 1.0pt{\mathfrak{f}}({\bar{z}})-{\bar{z}}^{2}(z-2)\hskip 1.0pt{\mathfrak{f}}(z)\bigr)}{z-{\bar{z}}}\,.

(4.9)

In a way, this is the same as making a choice of the vector
\vec{w}
introduced in subsection
3.3
.
It can be checked that the action of
\chi
and all of its variants — see (

) — acting on the expression above yield
{\mathfrak{f}}
.

4.2.3
Ward identity and ambiguity

Since the entire correlator is protected, the analysis that follows here is inessential. We will however show the steps in preparation to the subsequent cases where they become substantially more computationally involved. The Ward identities follow from requiring that the chiral algebra map
\chi
applied on the interacting correlator gives the same result as (
4.8
). This forces a generic unprotected contribution to
g_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})
, let us call it
\delta\hskip-0.8ptg_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})
, to satisfy

\delta\hskip-0.8ptg_{1}(z,{\bar{z}})=0\,,\qquad\delta\hskip-0.8ptg_{3}(z,{\bar{z}})=-\delta\hskip-0.8ptg_{2}(z,{\bar{z}})\,.

(4.10)

Following the notation introduced in subsection
3.3
, this fact can be encoded by defining the vectors
\vec{v}^{\hskip 1.0pt(m)}
and
\vec{w}
as follows:

\displaystyle\vec{v}^{\,(1)}

\displaystyle=\{0,1,-1\}\,,

(4.11)

\displaystyle\vec{w}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{N^{2}-4}}{\sqrt{3}\hskip 1.0pt(N^{2}-1)}\bigg\{\frac{z^{2}{\bar{z}}^{2}(z{\bar{z}}-z-{\bar{z}})}{(z-1)({\bar{z}}-1)},\,0,\,\frac{2z{\bar{z}}\left(z^{2}{\bar{z}}^{2}-z^{2}{\bar{z}}-z{\bar{z}}^{2}+2z+2{\bar{z}}-2\right)}{(z-1)({\bar{z}}-1)}\bigg\}\,.

There is only one ambiguity
\mathcal{A}_{1}(z,{\bar{z}})
. In this case, since the correlator is protected, we know that

\mathcal{A}_{1}=\frac{\sqrt{N^{2}-4}}{\sqrt{3}\hskip 1.0pt(N^{2}-1)}\frac{z^{2}{\bar{z}}^{2}(z{\bar{z}}-z-{\bar{z}}+2)}{(z-1)({\bar{z}}-1)}\,.

(4.12)

and
\mathcal{H}_{1}=0
. However, the criteria described in subsection
3.3
would not have allowed us to conclude this because there are no twist-two operators that need to be cancelled.

By expanding
\vec{w}
in conformal blocks we see that in the
(1,2,1)
we exchange only twist four while in the
(0,2,0)
we exchange
\mathcal{O}_{2}
and other operators of twist four. In detail we have

\displaystyle w_{1}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{N^{2}-4}}{\sqrt{3}(N^{2}-1)}\sum_{\ell=1,\,\mathrm{odd}}^{\infty}\frac{2^{\ell}\,\ell!\hskip 1.0pt(\ell+2)!}{(2\ell+1)!}\,g_{\ell+4,\ell}^{2,0}\,,

(4.13)

\displaystyle w_{3}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{N^{2}-4}}{\sqrt{3}\hskip 1.0pt(N^{2}-1)}\bigg(-4\hskip 1.0ptg_{2,0}^{2,0}+\sum_{\ell=0,\,\mathrm{even}}^{\infty}\frac{2^{\ell+1}\,\ell!\hskip 1.0pt(\ell+2)!}{(2\ell+1)!}\,g_{\ell+4,\ell}^{2,0}\bigg)\,.

4.3.2
Free theory

The free theory result can be written as follows

\langle\mathcal{O}_{02}(x_{1},{\mathbf{S}}_{1})\mathcal{O}_{02}(x_{2},{\mathbf{S}}_{2})\mathcal{O}_{2}(x_{3},y_{3})\mathcal{O}_{2}(x_{4},y_{4})\rangle={\hyperlink{eqref:kinPref}{\mathcal{K}_{4422}}}\,\sum_{k=1}^{10}g_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})\,{\hyperlink{eqref:TQQHHDef}{\mathbb{T}_{k}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptII}}}}\,,

(4.15)

Again, we will are not providing the explicit expression of the functions
g_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})
. In table
4

of the correlator

The result of acting with the chiral algebra map
\chi
reads

\frac{\chi\big[\langle\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle\big]}{{\hyperlink{eqref:kinPrefz}{\mathfrak{K}_{4422}}}}\equiv{\mathfrak{f}}_{\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}}(z)=\frac{3}{12}+\frac{N^{2}-4}{6\hskip 1.0pt(N^{2}-1)}\left(z^{2}+\frac{z^{2}}{(z-1)^{2}}\right)\,.

(4.16)

4.3.3
Ward identity and ambiguity

The Ward identities are specified by an uplift vector
\vec{w}
and eight ambiguity vectors
\vec{v}^{\,(m)}
.
The nonzero entries of the vector
\vec{w}
read

\displaystyle w_{9}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=-\frac{N^{2}-4}{N^{2}-1}\left(\hat{w}_{9}(z,{\bar{z}})+\hat{w}_{9}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.09}{\hbox{$\left(\vbox to13.5pt{}\right.$}}}}}\frac{z}{z-1},\frac{{\bar{z}}}{{\bar{z}}-1}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.09}{\hbox{$\left)\vbox to13.5pt{}\right.$}}}}}\right)\,,

(4.17)

\displaystyle w_{10}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=1+\frac{N^{2}-4}{N^{2}-1}\left(\hat{w}_{10}(z,{\bar{z}})-\hat{w}_{10}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.09}{\hbox{$\left(\vbox to13.5pt{}\right.$}}}}}\frac{z}{z-1},\frac{{\bar{z}}}{{\bar{z}}-1}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.09}{\hbox{$\left)\vbox to13.5pt{}\right.$}}}}}\right)\,,

where we have defined

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{9}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=\frac{8}{3}\big(z^{2}{\bar{z}}+z{\bar{z}}^{2}\big)\,,

(4.18)

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{10}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}\big(z^{2}{\bar{z}}+z{\bar{z}}^{2}-2z{\bar{z}}\big)\,.

The expressions for the ambiguity vectors instead are given by

v^{(m)}_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})=\delta^{m}_{k}+\hat{v}^{(m)}_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})\,,\qquad m=1,\hskip 1.0pt\ldots,\hskip 1.0pt8\,,

(4.19)

with the nonzero entries of
\hat{v}^{(m)}_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})
being

\displaystyle-2\hat{v}^{(1)}_{10}=\hat{v}^{(7)}_{10}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{30}\,,

(4.20)

\displaystyle\hat{v}^{(3)}_{9}

\displaystyle=2\,,

\displaystyle-\hat{v}^{(4)}_{9}=\hat{v}^{(5)}_{9}=\hat{v}^{(6)}_{9}

\displaystyle=\frac{8\hskip 1.0pt(z+{\bar{z}}-z{\bar{z}})}{3\hskip 1.0ptz{\bar{z}}}\,,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{30}-\hat{v}^{(4)}_{10}=\hat{v}^{(5)}_{10}=\hat{v}^{(6)}_{10}

\displaystyle=\frac{3z{\bar{z}}-5z-5{\bar{z}}+10}{15\hskip 1.0ptz{\bar{z}}}\,.

As before, we can expand the uplift vector in conformal blocks to see what are the contributions coming from the chiral algebra. We obtain the following result

\displaystyle w_{9}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=\frac{N^{2}-4}{3(N^{2}-1)}\sum_{\ell=1,\,\mathrm{odd}}^{\infty}\frac{2^{\ell+3}\hskip 1.0pt\ell!\hskip 1.0pt(\ell+1)!}{(2\ell-1)!}g_{\ell+2,\ell}\,,

(4.21)

\displaystyle w_{10}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=g_{0,0}-\frac{N^{2}-4}{3(N^{2}-1)}\sum_{\ell=0,\,\mathrm{even}}^{\infty}\frac{2^{\ell}\hskip 1.0pt\ell\hskip 1.0pt(\ell^{2}+\ell+2)\hskip 1.0pt\big((\ell-1)!\big)^{2}}{(2\ell-1)!}g_{\ell+2,\ell}\,.

The above expressions show the presence of a tower of higher spin conserved currents in both protected structures. As we remarked in section
3.3
, these should disappear in the interacting theory. We therefore have to tweak the ambiguity such that all the twist-two contributions vanish with the exception of spin zero, one and two, which all belong to the
\mathcal{O}_{2}
multiplet. Furthermore we also have to make sure that the OPE coefficient of
g_{2,0}
in the
(0,2,0)
is not modified. As before, in order to achieve this in practice we make an ansatz with a finite number of blocks for the ambiguities
\mathcal{A}_{m}
, we impose that the twist-two contributions vanish up to a certain maximal spin
\ell_{\mathrm{max}}
, and then we extrapolate our results for infinite
\ell_{\mathrm{max}}
.

The conjugation properties of the structures
T

k

II

allow us to set to zero
\mathcal{A}_{8}
and also to set
\mathcal{A}_{5}=\mathcal{A}_{6}
. Furthermore, since
\mathcal{A}_{4}
and
\mathcal{A}_{5}
exchange the same representation, we can assume that they will be proportional to each other. After these remarks we can fix the twist-four contributions of
\mathcal{A}_{4}
,
\mathcal{A}_{5}
and
\mathcal{A}_{6}
up to a single constant
\kappa
and the twist-two contribution of
\mathcal{A}_{7}
. On the other hand, we cannot say anything about
\mathcal{A}_{1}
,
\mathcal{A}_{2}
and
\mathcal{A}_{3}
. Presumably the knowledge of the superconformal blocks would allow us to fix their twist-four sector as well, but at the moment we do not have access to this information. All in all, the ambiguities that could be fixed read

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{5}=\mathcal{A}_{6}=\frac{1}{\kappa}\mathcal{A}_{4}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\hskip 1.0pt(N^{2}-3)}{(\kappa-2)(N^{2}-1)}g_{4,0}-\frac{N^{2}-4}{N^{2}-1}\sum_{\ell=2,\,\mathrm{even}}^{\infty}\frac{2^{\ell}\hskip 1.0pt(\ell+1)!(\ell+2)!}{(2\ell+1)!(\kappa-2)}\,g_{\ell+4,\ell}\,,

(4.22)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{7}

\displaystyle=-\frac{40}{N^{2}-1}\,g_{2,0}\,.

The sum over spins can be performed with the methods described in appendix
E.2
. The result is

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{5}(z,{\bar{z}})=\frac{N^{2}-4}{(N^{2}-1)(\kappa-2)}\bigg[

\displaystyle a(z,{\bar{z}})\hskip 1.0pt\log(1-{\bar{z}})+a({\bar{z}},z)\hskip 1.0pt\log(1-z)

(4.23)

\displaystyle-24\log(1-z)\log(1-{\bar{z}})\bigg]-\frac{2\hskip 1.0pt(N^{2}-3)}{(\kappa-2)(N^{2}-1)}g_{4,0}\,,

with

a(z,{\bar{z}})=\frac{z{\bar{z}}}{z-{\bar{z}}}\left(\hat{a}(z)+\hat{a}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.09}{\hbox{$\left(\vbox to13.5pt{}\right.$}}}}}\frac{z}{z-1}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.09}{\hbox{$\left)\vbox to13.5pt{}\right.$}}}}}\right)\,,\qquad\hat{a}(z)=12+z^{2}\,.

(4.24)

Notice that, while we chose
\mathcal{A}_{7}
such that the OPE coefficient of
\tr(y\cdot\varphi)^{2}
matches the free theory value, we did not do that for the other twist-two operators, namely the R-current
J_{\mu}
and the stress-tensor
T_{\mu\nu}
. Indeed their free theory values extracted naively are incorrect as they are contaminated by superdescentants of the free Konishi operator
\tr\varphi^{2}=A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}[0;0]^{(0,0,0)}
which are lifted in the interacting theory. More precisely, at levels
2l=2
and
2l=4
the Konishi operator has a “fake” R-current and stress tensor descendant, respectively:
Q^{l}{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}{}^{\hskip 0.5ptl}\,\tr\varphi^{2}
. The true values differ from the free theory ones by a simple multiplicative factor

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}J_{\mu}}\lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}J_{\mu}}=\frac{1}{3}\,\lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}J_{\mu}}^{\mathrm{free}}\lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}J_{\mu}}^{\mathrm{free}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{32}{3(N^{2}-1)}\,,

(4.25a)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}T_{\mu\nu}}\lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}T_{\mu\nu}}=\frac{1}{5}\,\lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}T_{\mu\nu}}^{\mathrm{free}}\lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}T_{\mu\nu}}^{\mathrm{free}}

\displaystyle=\frac{16}{45(N^{2}-1)}\,,

(4.25b)

where the OPE coefficient of the current is taken from the structure
\mathbb{T}_{9}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptII}}
, the other structure in the
(1,0,1)
being zero.
Getting the correct OPE coefficient for the twist-two contributions will be crucial for the results of section
6
because they give the only singular contribution to the crossed correlator, which is consequently the only one picked up the
\mathrm{dDisc}
.

4.4
Correlator
\boldsymbol{\langle\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle}

and the associated tensor structures are given below.

4.4.3
Ward identity and ambiguity

The Ward identities are specified by an uplift vector
\vec{w}
and eight ambiguity vectors
\vec{v}^{\,(m)}
. The nonzero entries of the vector
\vec{w}
read

\displaystyle w_{6}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=\frac{z^{2}{\bar{z}}^{2}}{(z-1)^{2}({\bar{z}}-1)^{2}}\left(\frac{3\,(2z{\bar{z}}-z-{\bar{z}})}{2\,(z-1)^{2}({\bar{z}}-1)^{2}}-\frac{N^{2}-4}{N^{2}-1}(z+{\bar{z}})\right)\,,

(4.30)

\displaystyle w_{10}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=\frac{7\hskip 1.0ptz{\bar{z}}}{(z-1)^{2}({\bar{z}}-1)^{2}}\bigg(\frac{3\hskip 1.0ptz{\bar{z}}\,(10z{\bar{z}}+7z+7{\bar{z}}-24)}{2\,(z-1)^{2}({\bar{z}}-1)^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{N^{2}-4}{N^{2}-1}(7z^{2}{\bar{z}}+7z{\bar{z}}^{2}-24z{\bar{z}}+24)\bigg)\,.

The ambiguity vectors instead can be expressed as follows

\displaystyle v^{(m)}_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=\delta^{m}_{k}+\hat{v}^{(m)}_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})\,,

\displaystyle\qquad m

\displaystyle=1,\,\ldots,\,5\,,

(4.31)

\displaystyle v^{(m)}_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=\delta^{m}_{k+1}+\hat{v}^{(m)}_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})\,,

\displaystyle\qquad m

\displaystyle=6,\,7,\,8\,,

with the nonzero entries of
\hat{v}^{(m)}_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})
being

\displaystyle\hat{v}_{6}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{3(4z{\bar{z}}-5z-5{\bar{z}})}{10z{\bar{z}}}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\hat{v}_{10}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{3(506z{\bar{z}}-1225z-1225{\bar{z}}+4200)}{50z{\bar{z}}}\,,

\displaystyle\hat{v}_{6}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{5}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\hat{v}_{10}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{214}{25}\,,

\displaystyle\hat{v}_{6}^{(3)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{5}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\hat{v}_{10}^{(3)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{321}{25}\,,

\displaystyle\hat{v}_{10}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=-14\,,

\displaystyle\hat{v}_{10}^{(6)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{63}{25}\,,

\displaystyle\hat{v}_{10}^{(7)}

\displaystyle=-9\,,

\displaystyle\hat{v}_{10}^{(8)}

\displaystyle=54\,.

(4.32)

From the vector
\vec{w}
we can obtain the conformal blocks expansion of the protected sector. Here is the result

\displaystyle w_{6}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\,\left(\frac{N^{2}-4}{N^{2}-1}c_{6}+d_{6}\right)g_{\ell+4,\ell}^{-2,2}\,,

(4.33)

\displaystyle w_{10}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=\frac{168(N^{2}-4)}{N^{2}-1}\,g_{2,0}^{-2,2}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\,\left(\frac{N^{2}-4}{N^{2}-1}c_{10}+d_{10}\right)g_{\ell+4,\ell}^{-2,2}\,.

with

\displaystyle c_{6}

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{\ell-1}\,\ell\,\ell!\hskip 1.0pt(\ell+3)!}{(\ell+1)(2\ell+1)!}\,,

(4.34)

\displaystyle d_{6}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{\ell+1}2^{\ell-3}\,\ell\hskip 1.0pt(\ell+2)\,\ell!\hskip 1.0pt(\ell+3)!}{(2\ell+1)!}\,,

\displaystyle c_{10}

\displaystyle=-\frac{7\cdot 2^{\ell-1}(\ell+2)\big(7\hskip 1.0pt\ell\hskip 1.0pt(\ell+3)+24\big)(\ell!)^{2}}{(2\ell+1)!}\,,

\displaystyle d_{10}

\displaystyle=\frac{7\cdot 2^{\ell-3}(-1)^{\ell+1}(\ell+1)(\ell+2)^{2}\big(17\hskip 1.0pt\ell\hskip 1.0pt(\ell+3)+72\big)(\ell!)^{2}}{(2\ell+1)!}\,.

The only twist-two operator exchanged is precisely
\mathcal{O}_{2}
since the representation exchanged in the structure
T

10

III

is the
(0,2,0)
. One can check that the coefficient is the same as in the free theory so we do not have to modify anything. The other contributions are of twist four and therefore we cannot say anything about the ambiguities
\mathcal{A}_{m}
.

4.5
Correlator
\boldsymbol{\langle\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}\rangle}

The correlator
\langle\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}\rangle
has 42 different structures exchanging 19 distinct representations.
The label assignment we chose is summarized in table

\mathbb{S}^{i_{1}j_{1}k_{1}l_{1}}_{i_{2}j_{2}k_{2}l_{2}}\equiv\prod_{n=1,2}\mathsf{S}_{1i_{n}}\mathsf{S}_{2j_{n}}\mathsf{S}_{3k_{n}}\mathsf{S}_{4l_{n}}\,.

(4.35)

For example
\mathbb{S}^{2143}_{2143}=(\mathsf{S}_{12})^{2}(\mathsf{S}_{21})^{2}(\mathsf{S}_{34})^{2}(\mathsf{S}_{43})^{2}
.

4.5.2
Free theory

The free theory result can be written as follows

\langle\mathcal{O}_{02}(x_{1},{\mathbf{S}}_{1})\mathcal{O}_{02}(x_{2},{\mathbf{S}}_{2})\mathcal{O}_{02}(x_{3},{\mathbf{S}}_{3})\mathcal{O}_{02}(x_{4},{\mathbf{S}}_{4})\rangle={\hyperlink{eqref:kinPref}{\mathcal{K}_{4444}}}\,\sum_{k=1}^{42}g_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})\,{\hyperlink{eqref:TQQQQDef}{\mathbb{T}_{k}^{\mathrm{IV}}}}\,,

(4.37)

Computing the free theory value of this correlator is somewhat challenging because each operator is a sum of two terms each being a product of four fields (
4.4
). The number of Wick contractions grows factorially and furthermore one has to take the traces over
\mathrm{SU}(N)
and
\mathrm{SO}(6)
indices. In practice we computed the correlators with
n

\mathcal{O}_{02}^{(\mathrm{dt})}
and
4-n

\mathcal{O}_{02}^{(\mathrm{st},1)}
, in some ordering, and then found the full correlator by permuting the points in the results.

(4.39)

The rational function of
N
above will appear in the next subsection as well, so let us define a shorthand for it

R_{N}\equiv\frac{N^{4}-7N^{2}+13}{(N^{2}-4)(N^{2}-1)}\,.

(4.40)

4.5.3
Ward identity and ambiguity

The Ward identities are specified by an uplift vector
\vec{w}
and 40 ambiguity vectors
\vec{v}^{\,(m)}
. The nonzero entries of the vector
\vec{w}
read

\displaystyle w_{41}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=336\left(\hat{w}_{41}(z,{\bar{z}})-\hat{w}_{41}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.09}{\hbox{$\left(\vbox to13.5pt{}\right.$}}}}}\frac{z}{z-1},\frac{{\bar{z}}}{{\bar{z}}-1}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.09}{\hbox{$\left)\vbox to13.5pt{}\right.$}}}}}\right)\,,

(4.41)

\displaystyle w_{42}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=1+\hat{w}_{42}(z,{\bar{z}})-\hat{w}_{42}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.09}{\hbox{$\left(\vbox to13.5pt{}\right.$}}}}}\frac{z}{z-1},\frac{{\bar{z}}}{{\bar{z}}-1}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.09}{\hbox{$\left)\vbox to13.5pt{}\right.$}}}}}\,,

where we have introduced two functions which, calling
\hat{z}\equiv z+{\bar{z}}
and using
R_{N}
from (
4.40
), read

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{41}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=z{\bar{z}}\hat{z}\hskip 1.0pt\bigl(z^{2}+{\bar{z}}^{2}\bigr)+\frac{8}{3}\hskip 1.0ptR_{N}\hskip 1.0ptz{\bar{z}}\hskip 1.0pt(\hat{z}+1)\,,

(4.42)

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{42}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.0ptz{\bar{z}}\,\bigl(\hat{z}^{3}-2\hskip 1.0pt\hat{z}^{2}-2\hskip 1.0ptz{\bar{z}}\hskip 1.0pt(\hat{z}-1)\bigr)+\frac{4}{3}\hskip 1.0ptR_{N}\hskip 1.0ptz{\bar{z}}\hskip 1.0pt(\hat{z}-1)\,.

The ambiguity vectors instead can be expressed as follows

v^{(m)}_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})=\delta^{m}_{k}+\hat{v}^{(m)}_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})\,.

(4.43)

The expressions of the nonzero entries of
\hat{v}^{(m)}_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})
are quite lengthy so we report them in appendix
B
. From the vector
\vec{w}
we can obtain the conformal blocks expansion of the protected sector. Here is the result
27
27

27

(\ell-1)_{n}=(\ell-1)\ell(\ell+1)\cdots(\ell+n-2)
is the Pochhammer symbol.

\displaystyle w_{41}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1,\,\mathrm{odd}}^{\infty}\frac{7}{3}\hskip 1.0pt\frac{2^{\ell+2}\hskip 1.0pt\ell!\hskip 1.0pt(\ell-1)!}{(2\ell-1)!}\left((\ell-2)_{6}+96\hskip 1.0ptR_{N}\hskip 1.0pt\big(\ell^{2}+\ell-1\big)\right)g_{\ell+2,\ell}\,,

(4.44)

\displaystyle w_{42}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=g_{0,0}-\sum_{\ell=0,\,\mathrm{even}}^{\infty}\frac{2^{\ell}\hskip 1.0pt(\ell!)^{2}}{3\hskip 1.0pt(2\ell)!}\left(\frac{(\ell-1)_{4}\hskip 1.0pt(\ell^{2}+\ell+12)\hskip 1.0pt}{12}+8\hskip 1.0ptR_{N}\hskip 1.0pt\big(\ell^{2}+\ell+1\big)\right)g_{\ell+2,\ell}\,,

with
R_{N}
defined in (
4.40
).

Here, as in the
\langle\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle
case, we have to resolve an ambiguity. Indeed there is a tower of twist-two operators coming from the protected function
{\mathfrak{f}}
. In order to resolve this ambiguity we impose that the twist-two contributions completely vanish and also that the OPE coefficient of
g_{2,0}
in the
(0,2,0)
is the same as the free theory one. Furthermore, we can make an assumption similar to the one made before. Namely, since structures
26
–
34
are associated to the same representation, we can assume their contributions to the ambiguity to be proportional to each other. Actually, structures
28
and
31
can be set to zero altogether since their tensor structures
T

28

IV

and
T

31

IV

are purely imaginary under complex conjugation. Furthermore, if we assume also structure
34
to be proportional to the others, the resolution of the ambiguity requires it to be set to zero, so we will directly omit
\mathcal{A}_{34}
from the ansatz below.
28
28

28

One can see from table
8
that this structure is different as it exchanges operators from twist six onward.
This does not fix all the structures and leaves us with a large ambiguity. We will however see in subsection
6.2
that some OPE data can still be fixed unambiguously.

After the above remarks, we find that a minimal ansatz for the ambiguity reads as follows

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{33}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=a^{(33)}_{4,0}\,g_{4,0}+\sum_{\ell=2,\,\mathrm{even}}^{\infty}a^{(33)}_{\ell+4,\ell}\,g_{\ell+4,\ell}\,,

(4.45)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{m}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=\lambda_{m}\,a^{(33)}_{4,0}\,g_{4,0}+\kappa_{m}\sum_{\ell=2,\,\mathrm{even}}^{\infty}\,a^{(33)}_{\ell+4,\ell}\,g_{\ell+4,\ell}\,,\qquad m=26,\,27,\,29,\,30,\,32,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{37}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{3}\frac{7!}{N^{2}-1}\,g_{2,0}\,.

for some constants
\kappa_{m}
and
\lambda_{m}
— we have defined two independent sets of constants for the spin zero and spin
\ell\geq 2
part since the former does not follow the same pattern of the latter. The coefficients
a^{(m)}_{\Delta,\ell}
that follow from imposing the points discussed in subsection
3.3
are given by

\displaystyle a^{(33)}_{4,0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{143\cdot(5!)^{2}\hskip 1.0pt(N^{2}-3)^{2}}{\mathcal{D}(\{\lambda_{m}\})\hskip 1.0pt(N^{2}-4)(N^{2}-1)}\,,

(4.46)

\displaystyle a^{(33)}_{4+\ell,\ell}

\displaystyle=-\frac{21450}{\mathcal{D}(\{\kappa_{m}\})}\frac{2^{\ell}\hskip 1.0pt\ell!(\ell+1)!}{(2\ell+1)!}\left(3\cdot 2^{5}\big(\ell^{2}+3\ell+1\big)\hskip 1.0ptR_{N}+(\ell-1)_{6}\right)\,,\qquad\ell\geq 2\,,

with

\mathcal{D}(\{\kappa_{m}\})=1662-19305\hskip 1.0pt\kappa_{26}+77220\hskip 1.0pt\kappa_{27}+77220\hskip 1.0pt\kappa_{29}-64922\hskip 1.0pt\kappa_{30}-3198\hskip 1.0pt\kappa_{32}\,.

(4.47)

As before, this expression can be resummed using the methods of appendix
E.2
, leading to

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{33}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=a^{(33)}_{4,0}\,g_{4,0}+\frac{1}{\mathcal{D}(\{\kappa_{m}\})}\Big[\hskip-6.99997pt

\displaystyle\frac{z{\bar{z}}}{z-{\bar{z}}}{\hbox{$\left(\vbox to10.0pt{}\right.$}}a(z,{\bar{z}})+R_{N}\,b(z,{\bar{z}})-a({\bar{z}},z)-R_{N}\,b({\bar{z}},z){\hbox{$\left)\vbox to10.0pt{}\right.$}}

(4.48)

\displaystyle-1716\cdot(5!)^{2}\hskip 1.0ptR_{N}\log(1-z)\log(1-{\bar{z}})\Big]\,,

with

\displaystyle a(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=-21450\cdot(3!)^{2}\left(\hat{a}({\bar{z}})+\hat{a}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.09}{\hbox{$\left(\vbox to13.5pt{}\right.$}}}}}\frac{{\bar{z}}}{{\bar{z}}-1}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.09}{\hbox{$\left)\vbox to13.5pt{}\right.$}}}}}\right)\log(1-z)\,,

(4.49)

\displaystyle b(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=-143\cdot(5!)^{2}\left(12+\hat{b}({\bar{z}})+\hat{b}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.09}{\hbox{$\left(\vbox to13.5pt{}\right.$}}}}}\frac{{\bar{z}}}{{\bar{z}}-1}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.09}{\hbox{$\left)\vbox to13.5pt{}\right.$}}}}}\right)\log(1-z)\,,

\displaystyle\hat{a}(z)

\displaystyle=\,z^{4}\,,

\displaystyle\hat{b}(z)

\displaystyle=\big(z+z^{2}\big)\,.

The requirements discussed in subsection
3.3
also impose that
\mathcal{A}_{40}
exchanges no twist-two operators at all. The other components remain in principle arbitrary, including possibly contributions to
\mathcal{A}_{26},\ldots,\mathcal{A}_{34}
that have twist higher than four and thus not proportional to
\mathcal{A}_{33}
found above.

Just like the discussion before equation (
4.25
), also here the OPE coefficients of the stress tensor and R-current get a simple multiplicative factor with respect to their free theory naive value

\displaystyle(\lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}J_{\mu}})^{2}=\frac{1}{3}\,(\lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}J_{\mu}}^{\mathrm{free}})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{7\cdot 2^{8}}{N^{2}-1}\,,

(4.50a)

\displaystyle(\lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}T_{\mu\nu}})^{2}=\frac{1}{5}\,(\lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}T_{\mu\nu}}^{\mathrm{free}})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{32}{45(N^{2}-1)}\,,

(4.50b)

where the OPE coefficient of the current is taken from the structure
\mathbb{T}_{41}^{\mathrm{IV}}
, while the other structures in the
(1,0,1)
do not have any twist-two contribution. As before, this is a consequence of carefully studying the constraints stemming from the ambiguity resolution. In this case the twist-two operators are not the only ones giving a singular contribution in the crossed channel, and therefore the anomalous dimensions may depend on other unknown terms. It is still true, however, that the large spin asymptotics is fixed and it depends on these factors of
1/3
and
1/5
.

Section 5
Lorentzian inversion formula

5.1
Review of the Lorentzian inversion formula

Let us consider again a generic four-point function of quarter-BPS and/or half-BPS operators as in (
3.18
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(z,{\bar{z}},{\mathbf{S}}_{1},\,\ldots,\,{\mathbf{S}}_{4})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\mathrm{str}}}g_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})\;\mathbb{T}_{k}({\mathbf{S}}_{1},\,\ldots,\,{\mathbf{S}}_{4})\,

(5.1)

\displaystyle g_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\Delta,\ell}a_{k,\Delta,\ell}\hskip 1.0ptg_{\Delta,\ell}^{\Delta_{12}\Delta_{34}}(z,{\bar{z}})\,.

The Lorentzian inversion formula
Caron-Huot:2017vep
allows us to extract the
s
-channel OPE data directly from the double discontinuity of the correlator in terms of the sum of two functions, analytic
in the spin, depending on the two other channels contributions

\displaystyle c_{k}(\Delta,\ell)

\displaystyle=c^{t}_{k}(\Delta,\ell)+(-1)^{\ell}c^{u}_{k}(\Delta,\ell)\,,

(5.2a)

\displaystyle c^{t}_{k}(\Delta,\ell)

\displaystyle=\begin{aligned} &\frac{\tilde{\kappa}^{(\Delta_{12},\Delta_{34})}_{\Delta+\ell}(-2)^{\ell}}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{dz}{z^{2}}\frac{d{\bar{z}}}{{\bar{z}}^{2}}\left[(1-z)(1-{\bar{z}})\right]^{\frac{\Delta_{34}-\Delta_{12}}{2}}\times\\
&\times\kappa_{4-2h}^{\Delta_{12},\Delta_{34}}(z)\hskip 1.0pt\kappa_{2h+2\ell}^{\Delta_{12},\Delta_{34}}({\bar{z}})\,\text{dDisc}\left[\frac{{\bar{z}}-z}{z{\bar{z}}}g_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})\right]\,,\end{aligned}

(5.2b)

where we have introduced
h=\frac{\Delta-\ell}{2}
,
\tilde{\kappa}^{(r,s)}_{\beta}=\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\beta+r}{2}\right)\,\Gamma\left(\frac{\beta-r}{2}\right)\,\Gamma\left(\frac{\beta+s}{2}\right)\,\Gamma\left(\frac{\beta-s}{2}\right)}{2\pi^{2}\Gamma(\beta-1)\Gamma(\beta)}
and

\kappa_{\beta}^{(r,s)}(z)=z^{\beta/2}\hskip 1.0pt{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\frac{\beta-r}{2},\frac{\beta+s}{2};\beta;z\right)\,.

(5.3)

The function
c^{u}_{k}(\Delta,\ell)
can be easily obtained by its
t
-channel counterpart by replacing
\Delta_{1}\leftrightarrow\Delta_{2}
and
g_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})
with

\left((1-z)(1-{\bar{z}})\right)^{-\frac{\Delta_{34}}{2}}(\mathbb{M}_{1\leftrightarrow 2}^{\text{T}})_{kk^{\prime}}\left[g_{k^{\prime}}\left(\frac{z}{z-1},\frac{{\bar{z}}}{{\bar{z}}-1}\right)\right]_{\Delta_{1}\leftrightarrow\Delta_{2}}\,,

(5.4)

where
\mathbb{M}_{i\leftrightarrow j}
is the change of basis matrix between the original tensor structures and the one with the indices
i
and
j
exchanged

\mathbb{T}^{i\leftrightarrow j}_{k^{\prime}}=\left(\mathbb{M}_{i\leftrightarrow j}\right)_{k^{\prime}k}\mathbb{T}_{k}\,.

(5.5)

The function
c_{k}(\Delta,\ell)
encodes the OPE data: it is constructed in such a way that for fixed integer spins it develops poles in correspondence of the dimensions of the exchanged operators, whose residues represent the OPE coefficients according to

c_{k}(\Delta,\ell)\underset{\Delta\to\Delta_{\mathrm{ex}}}{\sim}\frac{a_{k,\Delta,\ell}}{\Delta_{\mathrm{ex}}-\Delta}\,.

(5.6)

Notice that the reconstruction of the OPE data through the inversion formula can be in general trusted only for spin
\ell\geq 2

Caron-Huot:2017vep
. However, in a large
N
expansion, higher orders can suffer of worse ambiguities thus invalidating the results for a larger though finite number of low spins
Alday:2017vkk
.
The double discontinuity appearing in (
5.2b
) is defined as the difference between the Euclidean correlators and its two possible analytic continuations around
{\bar{z}}=1
, namely

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \text{dDisc}\left[g_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})\right]&=\cos\left(\pi\alpha\right)g_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})-\frac{1}{2}e^{i\pi\alpha}g_{k}^{\circlearrowleft}(z,{\bar{z}})-\frac{1}{2}e^{-i\pi\alpha}g_{k}^{\circlearrowright}(z,{\bar{z}})\,,\\
\alpha&=\frac{\Delta_{34}-\Delta_{12}}{2}\,.\end{aligned}

(5.7)

Similarly to what was done in
Alday:2017vkk
;
Caron-Huot:2018kta
, in order to reconstruct the
s
-channel OPE we would like to employ only the information coming from the protected contributions in the cross-channels and in our case encoded in
w_{k}
of (
3.21
) together with the associated ambiguity (
3.23
). To do so, we will not consider directly the dDisc of the correlator as it is in (
5.2b
), but we will use crossing symmetry to re-express
g_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})
as

g_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})=\frac{(z{\bar{z}})^{\frac{\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}}{2}}}{\left((1-z)(1-{\bar{z}})\right)^{\frac{\Delta_{2}+\Delta_{3}}{2}}}(\mathbb{M}^{T}_{1\leftrightarrow 3})_{kk^{\prime}}\left[g_{k^{\prime}}(1-z,1-{\bar{z}})\right]_{\Delta_{1}\leftrightarrow\Delta_{3}}\,.

(5.8)

The same transformation has to be performed in
c^{u}_{k}
. At this stage, it is important to mention that differently from the all half-BPS case, we might not be able to construct the full tree-level correlator in this way. This is due to the fact that the only contribution we can completely fix is the one coming from twist-two operators and the identity, when present. Nonetheless we will try to draw some interesting conclusions about the anomalous dimensions and OPE coefficients of non-protected operators appearing in the OPE involving our quarter-BPS operators.

Let us now briefly review how the inversion formula is applied to our cases of interest. First of all, the only terms with non vanishing dDisc that we are going to encounter are

\displaystyle\text{dDisc}\left[\left(\frac{1-{\bar{z}}}{{\bar{z}}}\right)^{\lambda}\right]=\text{dDisc}[e^{\lambda\log\left(\frac{1-{\bar{z}}}{{\bar{z}}}\right)}]=\left(\frac{1-{\bar{z}}}{{\bar{z}}}\right)^{\lambda}2\sin(\pi\lambda)\sin(\pi(\lambda+\alpha))\,.

(5.9)

Also
\log(1-{\bar{z}})
appears in our expressions, but it is straightforward to see that its dDisc vanishes. All the relevant integrals appearing in
c^{t}_{k}(\Delta,\ell)
then are going to be of the form

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d{\bar{z}}}{{\bar{z}}^{2}}(1-{\bar{z}})^{\frac{\Delta_{34}-\Delta_{12}}{2}}\kappa_{2h+2\ell}^{\Delta_{12},\Delta_{34}}({\bar{z}})\hskip 1.0pt{\bar{z}}^{-\frac{\Delta_{34}}{2}}\text{dDisc}\left[\left(\frac{1-{\bar{z}}}{{\bar{z}}}\right)^{\lambda}\right]\times

(5.10)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{dz}{z^{2}}(1-z)^{\frac{\Delta_{34}-\Delta_{12}}{2}}\kappa_{4-2h}^{\Delta_{12},\Delta_{34}}(z)\hskip 1.0ptf(\boldsymbol{x}(\tilde{\lambda}),\boldsymbol{z}(\tilde{\lambda}),\log z)\,,

for some generic function
f
depending on the variables
\boldsymbol{x}(\tilde{\lambda})=z^{-\frac{\Delta_{34}}{2}}\big(\frac{z}{1-z}\big)^{\tilde{\lambda}}
,
\boldsymbol{z}(\tilde{\lambda})=z^{-\frac{\Delta_{34}}{2}}z^{\tilde{\lambda}}
and possible
\log z
. Explicit computations of these integrals can be found in appendix
D
. We will further consider a large central charge
c
expansion for our correlators. At
c\to\infty
the dimensions and OPE coefficients of non-protected operators acquire corrections with the respect to their bare values of the form
29
29

29

Notice that we have slightly changed notation, from
a^{(k)}_{\Delta,\ell}
to
a_{k,\Delta,\ell}
.

\displaystyle\Delta_{k}

\displaystyle=\Delta^{(0)}_{k}+\frac{\gamma^{(1)}_{k,\Delta,\ell}}{c}+\mathcal{O}(c^{-2})\,,

(5.11a)

\displaystyle a_{k,\Delta,\ell}

\displaystyle=a_{k,\Delta,\ell}^{(0)}+\frac{a_{k,\Delta,\ell}^{(1)}}{c}+\mathcal{O}(c^{-2})\,,

(5.11b)

where we have defined the anomalous dimension
\gamma^{(1)}_{k,\Delta,\ell}
. Accordingly, (
5.6
) gets expanded as

c_{k}(\Delta,\ell)\sim-\frac{1}{2}\bigg\langle\frac{a^{(0)}_{k,\Delta,\ell}}{h-\frac{\Delta^{(0)}-\ell}{2}}\bigg\rangle-\frac{1}{c}\Bigg(\frac{1}{4}\bigg\langle\frac{a^{(0)}_{k,\Delta,\ell}\gamma^{(1)}_{k,\Delta,\ell}}{\big(h-\frac{\Delta^{(0)}-\ell}{2}\big)^{\mbox{\hskip-0.8pt\tiny 2}}}\bigg\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\bigg\langle\frac{a^{(1)}_{k,\Delta,\ell}}{h-\frac{\Delta^{(0)}-\ell}{2}}\bigg\rangle\Bigg)+\mathcal{O}(c^{-2})\,,

(5.12)

where the brackets stand for averages over all the possible degenerate operators with the same twist. From this formula it is clear that at order
c^{0}
we expect simple poles for the
h
of the exchanged operators, whose OPE coefficients should recover the free theory results computed at
c\to\infty
. At the next order, double poles arise in correspondence of those operators developing an anomalous dimension. Notice that the information one can extract for this type of poles are not exactly the anomalous dimensions, but rather the products
\langle a^{(0)}_{k,\Delta,\ell}\gamma^{(1)}_{k,\Delta,\ell}\rangle
. Determining the
\gamma^{(1)}_{k,\Delta,\ell}
’s by themselves would indeed require solving a mixing problem at order
c^{0}
able to distinguish all the possible degeneracies. Finally the simple poles at order
c^{-1}
take into account the corrections to the OPE coefficients.

5.2
Half-BPS case

In the same spirit of subsection
3.4
, let us first review the well-known example of four half-BPS
\mathcal{O}_{2}
operators and let us apply the inversion formula to this correlator without relying on its expansion in superconformal blocks.

The way to proceed is the following: we start by considering the large
c
expansion of (
3.32
) and then we plug this in (
5.2b
) after passing to the crossed channel as explained before. The
u
-contribution can be obtained similarly. At order
c^{0}
we should be able to recover the free theory coefficients and indeed we find that the exchanged operators have twist four and higher and the corresponding
a^{(0)}_{k,\Delta,\ell}
are exactly the expected ones. After this preliminary check, we can try to extract corrections at order
c^{-1}
. The anomalous dimensions arise from those terms in the correlator proportional to
\log z
once passed to the
t
-channel. These are in fact the only contributions in (
5.10
) that can develop double poles. Let us denote
\Gamma^{k}_{h,\ell}=\langle a^{(0)}_{k,\Delta,\ell}\gamma^{(1)}_{k,\Delta,\ell}\rangle
, with
\Delta=2h+\ell
, for each tensor structure
k
in (
3.25
) and let us define

\Xi_{t}^{(\Delta_{12},\Delta_{34})}=\frac{\Gamma\left(t-\frac{\Delta_{12}}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(t+\frac{\Delta_{34}}{2}\right)}{4\Gamma\left(2t-1\right)}\,,\qquad\qquad\Xi_{t}\equiv\Xi_{t}^{(0,0)}\,.

(5.13)

Then our results read

\displaystyle\Gamma^{1}_{h,\ell}

\displaystyle=-\frac{4}{5}\hskip 1.0pt\Xi_{h+\ell}\Xi_{h-1}2^{\ell}(1+(\scalebox{0.75}[1.0]{$-$}1)^{\ell})\left(h^{4}-6h^{3}+27h^{2}-54h+\frac{136}{3}\right)

\displaystyle\text{for }h\geq 2\,,\ell\geq 4\,,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{2}_{h,\ell}

\displaystyle=-4\hskip 1.0pt\Xi_{h+\ell}\Xi_{h-1}2^{\ell}(1-(\scalebox{0.75}[1.0]{$-$}1)^{\ell})\left(h^{4}-6h^{3}+23h^{2}-42h+32\right)

\displaystyle\text{for }h\geq 2\,,\ell\geq 3\,,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{3}_{h,\ell}

\displaystyle=-\frac{8}{5}\hskip 1.0pt\Xi_{h,+\ell}\Xi_{h-1}2^{\ell}(1+(\scalebox{0.75}[1.0]{$-$}1)^{\ell})\left(h^{4}-6h^{3}+21h^{2}-36h+\frac{80}{3}\right)

\displaystyle\text{for }h\geq 2\,,\ell\geq 2\,,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{4}_{h,\ell}

\displaystyle=-\frac{16}{3}\hskip 1.0pt\Xi_{h+\ell}\Xi_{h-1}2^{\ell}(1+(\scalebox{0.75}[1.0]{$-$}1)^{\ell})(h-2)(h-1)\left(h^{2}-3h+6\right)

\displaystyle\text{for }h\geq 3\,,\ell\geq 2\,,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{5}_{h,\ell}

\displaystyle=-8\hskip 1.0pt\Xi_{h+\ell}\Xi_{h-1}2^{\ell}(1-(\scalebox{0.75}[1.0]{$-$}1)^{\ell})(h-2)(h-1)\left(h^{2}-3h+4\right)

\displaystyle\text{for }h\geq 3\,,\ell\geq 0\,,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{6}_{h,\ell}

\displaystyle=-\frac{8}{3}\hskip 1.0pt\Xi_{h+\ell}\Xi_{h-1}2^{\ell}(1+(\scalebox{0.75}[1.0]{$-$}1)^{\ell})(h-3)(h-2)(h-1)h

\displaystyle\text{for }h\geq 4\,,\ell\geq 0\,.

For all the other values of the twist the anomalous dimensions simply vanish, while for the remaining values of the spin our results do not agree with the ones of
Dolan:2001tt
.
30
30

30

This is not an inconsistency since we recall that the inversion formula can fail for low spins.
Notice that the exchange of either spin even or spin odd operators depends on the symmetry property of the corresponding tensor
\mathbb{T}_{k}
under the exchange
1\leftrightarrow 2
. To conclude, let us comment on the anomalous dimensions of the operators transforming in the
(0,4,0)
representation of SU(4), also known as
\mathbf{105}
, for which we do expect to recover the well known results — see for example
Alday:2017vkk
;
Caron-Huot:2018kta
. In this case we can write

\displaystyle\frac{\langle a^{(0)}_{6,\Delta,\ell}\gamma^{(1)}_{6,\Delta,\ell}\rangle}{\langle a^{(0)}_{6,\Delta,\ell}\rangle}=-\frac{(h-3)(h-2)(h-1)h}{(\ell+1)(2h+\ell-2)}\,,

(5.15)

which does agree with (2.13) of
Alday:2017vkk
modulo shifting
h\to h+2
and renaming
h=n+2
. At this same order the inversion formula also predicts corrections to the OPE coefficients. According to (
5.12
) these are encoded in the residue associated to the simple poles. Quite remarkably, they take a very simple form

\langle a_{k,\Delta,\ell}^{(1)}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{h}\Gamma^{k}_{h,\ell}\,,

(5.16)

with the exception of

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \langle a_{4,\Delta,\ell}^{(1)}\rangle&=-\frac{8}{3}\hskip 1.0pt\Xi_{2+\ell}(1+(\scalebox{0.75}[1.0]{$-$}1)^{\ell})&&\text{ for }h=2\,,\ell\geq 2\,,\\
\langle a_{5,\Delta,\ell}^{(1)}\rangle&=-2\hskip 1.0pt\Xi_{2+\ell}(1-(\scalebox{0.75}[1.0]{$-$}1)^{\ell})&&\text{ for }h=2\,,\ell\geq 0\,,\\
\langle a_{6,\Delta,\ell}^{(1)}\rangle&=\begin{cases}\frac{2}{3}\hskip 1.0pt\Xi_{2+\ell}(1+(\scalebox{0.75}[1.0]{$-$}1)^{\ell})\\
-\Xi_{3+\ell}(1+(\scalebox{0.75}[1.0]{$-$}1)^{\ell})\end{cases}&&\begin{aligned} \text{ for }h=2\,,\ell\geq 0\,,\\
\text{ for }h=3\,,\ell\geq 0\,.\\
\end{aligned}\end{aligned}

(5.17)

Section 6
Results

6.1
OPE data in the
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{O}_{2}\times\mathcal{O}_{02}}
OPE

The OPE data in the
\mathcal{O}_{2}\times\mathcal{O}_{02}
can be extracted by studying the correlator
\langle\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle
.
However, the method of the inversion formula outlined above requires the knowledge of the protected parts of its
u
- and
t
-crossed version. Therefore also
\langle\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle
will be needed. Explicitly, we have to consider

\displaystyle c^{t}_{k}(\Delta,\ell)

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{\kappa}^{(-2,2)}_{2h+2\ell}(-2)^{\ell}}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{dz}{z^{2}}\frac{d{\bar{z}}}{{\bar{z}}^{2}}\left[(1-z)(1-{\bar{z}})\right]^{2}\kappa_{4-2h}^{-2,2}(z)\hskip 1.0pt\kappa_{2h+2\ell}^{-2,2}({\bar{z}})\times

(6.1)

\displaystyle\times\text{dDisc}\left[\frac{{\bar{z}}-z}{z{\bar{z}}}\frac{(z{\bar{z}})^{3}}{((1-z)(1-{\bar{z}}))^{4}}\left(\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{III}\leftrightarrow\mathrm{II}}^{\text{T}}\right)_{kk^{\prime}}\mathcal{W}^{t}_{k^{\prime}}(1-z,1-{\bar{z}})\right]\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}^{t}_{k^{\prime}}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=w_{k^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\langle\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle}(z,{\bar{z}})+\mathcal{A}_{k^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\langle\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle}(z,{\bar{z}})\,,

where
\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{III}\leftrightarrow\mathrm{II}}
allows to pass from the
\mathbb{T}_{k}^{\mathrm{III}}
to the
\mathbb{T}_{k}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptII}}
basis. Similarly

\displaystyle c^{u}_{k}(\Delta,\ell)

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{\kappa}^{(2,2)}_{2h+2\ell}(-2)^{\ell}}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{dz}{z^{2}}\frac{d{\bar{z}}}{{\bar{z}}^{2}}\left[(1-z)(1-{\bar{z}})\right]^{2}\kappa_{4-2h}^{2,2}(z)\kappa_{2h+2\ell}^{2,2}({\bar{z}})\times

(6.2)

\displaystyle\times\text{dDisc}\left[\frac{{\bar{z}}-z}{z{\bar{z}}}\frac{(z{\bar{z}})^{3}}{((1-z)(1-{\bar{z}}))^{3}}\left(\mathbb{M}_{1\leftrightarrow 3}^{\text{T}}\right)_{kk^{\prime}}\mathcal{W}^{u}_{k^{\prime}}(1-z,1-{\bar{z}})\right]\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}^{u}_{k^{\prime}}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=(1-z)(1-{\bar{z}})\hskip 1.0ptw_{k^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\langle\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle}\left(\frac{z}{z-1},\frac{{\bar{z}}}{{\bar{z}}-1}\right)\,,

where in the third line we have used (
5.4
) with
\mathbb{M}_{1\leftrightarrow 2}=\mathds{1}
. Interestingly, by tracing back the contributions to
c^{t,u}_{k}(\Delta,\ell)
from
\mathcal{W}^{t,u}_{k}(z,{\bar{z}})
, one discovers that the only operators that contribute are the twist-two terms and the identity in
\langle\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle
, which we were able to completely fix. This consequently implies that there is no dependence on the undetermined constant
\kappa
.

Retracing the same steps of the all
\mathcal{O}_{2}
case, we have first of all checked that we were able to recover the disconnected OPE coefficients as given in the free theory.

Among all the possible representations exchanged in the OPE, the
(1,2,1)
seems rather special. Indeed

(6.4)

This observation suggests that this representation might be interpreted as the analogous of the
(0,4,0)
in the all
\mathcal{O}_{2}
case. Namely a representation where the superconformal block
32
32

32

By superconformal block in this context one usually means the familiar
u^{-2}g_{\Delta+4,\ell}
that appears in the unprotected part of the
\mathcal{O}_{2}
correlator. Here we are talking about the same object but expanded in the full four-point function. In the all
\mathcal{O}_{2}
case, all representations but the
(0,4,0)
have superblocks consisting of sums of conformal blocks, each associated to a different superdescedant. The
(0,4,0)
is the only representation where there is only a single superdescendant contribution, namely
Q^{4}{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}{}^{4}\mathcal{O}
, which has the same spin as the superprimary and the dimension is increased by four.
can be written as a
single
conformal block, possibly with shifted quantum numbers.

6.2
OPE data in the
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{O}_{02}\times\mathcal{O}_{02}}
OPE

The analysis for the OPE data contained in the
\mathcal{O}_{02}\times\mathcal{O}_{02}
OPE proceeds analogously, with the difference that in this case we will consider only
\langle\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}\rangle
. The relevant ingredients are given in subsection
4.5.3
. Another important difference with respect to the previous case is that the twist-two operators and the identity are not the only ones contributing to the dDisc, at order
c^{-1}
. This makes the answer not completely fixed and it introduces a dependence on the unknown coefficients
\kappa_{m}
,
\lambda_{m}
of equation (
4.45
). Remarkably, these constants do not appear in
c_{1}(\Delta,\ell)
: the function corresponding to the structure
(4,0,4)
. For this reason and to avoid clutter, we will report explicitly the anomalous dimension and
a^{(1)}_{k,\Delta,\ell}
just for this interesting case.
33
33

33

The results for all the other structures can be found in an ancillary
Mathematica
file attached to the arXiv version of this paper.
We find that, starting at twist
\tau=10
(
h\geq 5
) the operators exchanged in
\mathcal{O}_{02}\times\mathcal{O}_{02}
transforming in the
(4,0,4)
acquire an anomalous dimension

\displaystyle\langle a^{(0)}_{1,\Delta,\ell}\gamma^{(1)}_{1,\Delta,\ell}\rangle=-\frac{1}{9}(-2)^{\ell}\left(1+(\scalebox{0.75}[1.0]{$-$}1)^{\ell}\right)\Xi_{h+\ell}\Xi_{h-1}(h-4)_{6}\Big((h+\ell-2)_{4}(h^{2}-3h-2)

(6.6)

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{3}(16(h+\ell-1)(h+\ell)+(h-5)(h+2)(3(h+\ell-1)(h+\ell)-2)-8)\Big)\,.

The first term corresponds to the contributions coming from the identity and the twist-two operators and it is the leading one at large spin. The second term, on the other hand, may receive corrections from twist-four operators that we could not fix in the ambiguity — for instance the scalar of dimension four in the
(0,4,0)
, or
\mathbb{T}_{13}^{\mathrm{IV}}
. For this reason we quote the large spin asymptotic of the above result after having factorized away the free theory OPE coefficient

\frac{\langle a^{(0)}_{1,\Delta,\ell}\gamma^{(1)}_{1,\Delta,\ell}\rangle}{\langle a^{(0)}_{1,\Delta,\ell}\rangle}\xrightarrow{\ell\to\infty}-\frac{(h-4)(h+1)\left(h^{2}-3h-2\right)}{\ell^{2}}\qquad\text{for }h\geq 5\,.

(6.7)

6.3
A note about the MRV limit

The simplicity of the result (
6.7
), and the fact that it does not depend on the
\kappa_{m}
,
\lambda_{m}
constants, suggests that the representation
(4,0,4)
is the analog of the
(0,4,0)
in the all
\mathcal{O}_{2}
case, in the sense explained around footnote
32
. Also note that the anomalous dimensions start from twist ten, contrary to the ones in the other structures that start from twist eight, which is the twist that one expects for a
[\mathcal{O}_{02}\hskip 1.0pt\mathcal{O}_{02}]_{0,\ell}
operator. This is indicative of a shift in the dimension of the conformal block. Roughly speaking, this means that we expect the superconformal block in the structure
T

1

IV

to be proportional to
g_{\Delta+2,\ell}
.

The analogy with the all half-BPS case can be made even more precise here. Indeed we can devise an analog of the Maximally R-symmetry Violating (MRV) limit introduced in
Alday:2020lbp
. In the
s
-channel this limit consists in sending
y_{1}\to y_{2}
. In our more general case we have

y_{1}\to y_{2}\,,\qquad S_{1}\to S_{2}\,,\qquad{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muS\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{1}\to{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muS\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{2}\,.

(6.8)

Taking this limit on the tensor structures
T

k

IV

yields

\lim_{\mathrm{1\to 2}}{\hyperlink{eqref:TQQQQDef}{\mathbb{T}_{k}^{\mathrm{IV}}}}=\delta_{k1}\,(\mathsf{S}_{23})^{2}(\mathsf{S}_{32})^{2}(\mathsf{S}_{24})^{2}(\mathsf{S}_{42})^{2}\,,

(6.9)

namely it sends all structures to zero but the first, which is the one we are considering here. Even though we did not study these correlators in Mellin space, analogously to the findings of
Alday:2020lbp
we expect a zero in
u
at twist eight when taking the
u
-channel limit (which is
1\to 3
instead of
1\to 2
). Note that here we expect only one zero instead of two zeros.

We further note that the same reasoning will not work for the
\langle\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle
because no particular structure is isolated by taking the MRV limit. In that case however — as we have seen in section
6.1
— the anomalous dimensions start from twist six anyway, so we do not expect any additional zeros in Mellin space. Finally, taking the MRV limit (
6.8
) on
\langle\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle
precisely isolates the
(0,4,0)
representation. This is consistent with one’s expectations since the long multiplets in that OPE will be the same as those exchanged in the all half-BPS case.

Section 7
Double trace correlators from higher-point Witten diagrams

7.1
Five-point function and the OPE

In this section we want to prove that the correlator
\langle\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle
is protected at lowest nontrivial order in the
1/N
expansion. This is not surprising since this correlator is “next-to-extremal” but there is no available proof of it yet because the usual arguments only apply to the half-BPS case
Eden:2000gg
;
Erdmenger:1999pz
;
Baggio:2012rr
. In order to achieve that, we will look at the five-point function
\langle\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle
. The procedure will involve two steps: first we need to decompose the five-point function into tensor structures that correspond to the exchange of a given representation between the first two operators and secondly we need to take the OPE limit and extract the most singular piece. Since, in the interacting theory,
\mathcal{O}_{02}
is the lightest operator in the
(2,0,2)
sector of the
\mathcal{O}_{2}\times\mathcal{O}_{2}
OPE, the leading singularity as
x_{1}\to x_{2}
is guaranteed to give us the correlator that we need.

The five-point function was studied up to order
1/N^{3}
in the supergravity limit in a recent paper
Goncalves:2019znr
. Let us report here their results for convenience. The free theory value at finite
N
reads

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{O}_{2}(x_{1},y_{1})\ldots\mathcal{O}_{2}(x_{5},y_{5})\rangle\big|_{\mathrm{free}}\equiv\mathcal{G}^{(5)}_{\mathrm{free}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{5};y_{1},\ldots,y_{5})=

(7.1)

\displaystyle=2\sqrt{\frac{2}{N^{2}-1}}\Bigg[\sum_{\mathrm{perm}}\frac{\mathcal{A}_{ijk,lm}}{x_{ij}^{2}x_{jk}^{2}x_{ki}^{2}(x_{lm}^{2})^{2}}+\frac{2}{N^{2}-1}\sum_{\mathrm{perm}}\frac{\mathcal{A}_{ijklm}}{x_{ij}^{2}x_{jk}^{2}x_{kl}^{2}x_{lm}^{2}x_{mi}^{2}}\Bigg]\,,

where we defined

\mathcal{A}_{ijk,lm}=\mathsf{y}_{ij}\hskip 1.0pt\mathsf{y}_{jk}\hskip 1.0pt\mathsf{y}_{ki}\hskip 1.0pt\mathsf{y}_{lm}^{2}\,,\qquad\mathcal{A}_{ijklm}=\mathsf{y}_{ij}\hskip 1.0pt\mathsf{y}_{jk}\hskip 1.0pt\mathsf{y}_{kl}\hskip 1.0pt\mathsf{y}_{lm}\hskip 1.0pt\mathsf{y}_{mi}\,,

(7.2)

and the sums
\sum_{\mathrm{perm}}
are over all inequivalent permutations of five elements, each counted once. The large
N
result in the supergravity approximation (namely the ’t Hooft coupling
\lambda
being sent to infinity) instead is given by

\langle\mathcal{O}_{2}(x_{1},y_{1})\cdots\mathcal{O}_{2}(x_{5},y_{5})\rangle\big|_{\mathrm{sugra}}=\mathcal{G}^{(5)}_{\mathrm{free}}\big|_{1\over N}+\frac{1}{N^{3}}\mathcal{G}^{(5)}_{\mathrm{sugra}}\,,

(7.3)

where
\mathcal{G}^{(5)}_{\mathrm{sugra}}
is given in an auxiliary file of
Goncalves:2019znr
and is a linear combination of the following
D
-functions

\mathcal{G}^{(5)}_{\mathrm{sugra}}\supset\big\{D_{11123}\,,\;D_{11233}\,,\;D_{11112}\,,\;D_{11222}\,,\;\mathrm{permutations}\big\}\,.

(7.4)

The
D
-functions with
n
labels represent an
n
-point contact Witten diagram or, in other words, the integral of
n
bulk-to-boundary propagators

D_{\Delta_{1}\cdots\Delta_{n}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{z^{5}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\mathrm{d}^{4}x\,\prod^{n}_{i=1}\left(\frac{z}{z^{2}+(\vec{x}-\vec{x}_{i})^{2}}\right)^{\Delta_{i}}\,.

(7.5)

The four-point function we want to match is shown at zero coupling in equation (
4.7
). Since we only need to extract the most singular piece in the OPE we need only the following term

\mathcal{O}_{2}(x_{1},y_{1})\times\mathcal{O}_{2}(x_{2},y_{2})\big|_{(2,0,2)}=\lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{02}}\,\big(\mathsf{J}^{\partial_{3}\partial_{3}}_{12}\big)^{2}\mathcal{O}_{02}(x_{2},S_{3},{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muS\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{3})+O\big(x_{12}^{2}\big)\,.

(7.6)

Here we introduced a new notation: whenever a symbol
\partial_{i}
replaces a label
i
in a tensor structure — defined in (
2.18
) — we need to replace the polarization attached to that point with a covariant derivative — defined in (
A.7
)

i\to\partial_{i}\quad\equiv\quad y_{i\hskip 0.5ptM}\to\mathcal{D}_{i\hskip 1.0ptM}\,,\qquad S_{i}^{m}\to\bar{\partial}^{m}_{i}\,,\qquad S_{i\hskip 1.0ptm}\to\partial_{i\hskip 1.0ptm}\,.

(7.7)

As we remarked before, the differential operators defined in (
A.7
) can only be applied to
(q,0,{\bar{q}})
or
(0,p,0)
tensors. As we will explain in the next subsection, it is unnecessary to know their explicit expressions anyway because we can always adopt the Casimir method as in subsection
2.3.3
.

7.2
Projecting on R-symmetry representations

In order to find our operators of interest we need to decompose the tensor structures
\mathcal{A}_{ijklm}
and
\mathcal{A}_{ijk,lm}
into a basis of projectors associated to the representations exchanged in the
\mathcal{O}_{2}\times\mathcal{O}_{2}
OPE. Here it follows a systematic way to do it. In general the OPE can be written as

\mathcal{O}_{2}(x_{1},y_{1})\times\mathcal{O}_{2}(x_{2},y_{2})=\sum_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}\lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{L}}\,\frac{t^{\mathsf{OPE}}_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}(y_{1},y_{2},\boldsymbol{\partial}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{0}})}{(x_{12}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}(4-\Delta_{L})}}\mathcal{O}_{L}(x_{2},{\mathbf{S}}_{0})+O(x_{12}^{2})\,,

(7.8)

with
t^{\mathsf{OPE}}_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}
being a differential operator in the polarizations
{\mathbf{S}}_{0}
and the sum ranging over all lightest operators of dimension
\Delta_{L}
within a given representation. If we use this equation inside a five-point function we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{x_{1}\to x_{2}}(x_{12}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}(4-\Delta_{L})}\langle\mathcal{O}_{2}(x_{1},y_{1})\mathcal{O}_{2}(x_{2},y_{2})\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle

(7.9)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}\lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{L}}\,t^{\mathsf{OPE}}_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}(y_{1},y_{2},\boldsymbol{\partial}_{{\mathbf{S}}_{0}})\langle\mathcal{O}_{L}(x_{2},{\mathbf{S}}_{0})\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle\,.

Since
t^{\mathsf{OPE}}_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}
are essentially three-point functions, they satisfy the Casimir equation in the first two points

\mathcal{C}_{2}(\partial_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}},\partial_{{\mathbf{S}}_{2}})\,t^{\mathsf{OPE}}_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}(y_{1},y_{2};\partial_{{\mathbf{S}}_{0}})=C_{2}\,t^{\mathsf{OPE}}_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}(y_{1},y_{2};\partial_{{\mathbf{S}}_{0}})\,.

(7.10)

Therefore we can expand the five-point function
\langle\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle
into an arbitrary basis of monomials in
\mathsf{y}_{ij}
and then separate the various contributions by rotating into a basis which diagonalizes
\mathcal{C}_{2}
. In this particular case the quadratic Casimir is sufficient to fix all the structures. For the representations in which
pq=0
we can alternatively apply the differential operator directly on the four-point tensor structures. For example, considering
(2,0,2)
one has
34
34

34

The numerical factor in front is arbitrary. It was chosen to factor an overall constant multiplying (
7.13
).

t_{(2,0,2)}^{\mathsf{OPE}}(y_{1},y_{2},\partial_{S_{0}},\partial_{{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muS\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{0}})=\frac{1}{2^{9}3^{2}}\big(\mathsf{J}^{\partial_{0}\partial_{0}}_{12}\big)^{2}\,,

(7.11)

which should act on the three different four-point structures of
\langle\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle
. Here we pick a simpler linear combination of the
T

k

I

of (
4.6
) since we do not care about the representations exchanged in the rest of the four-point function — note that we shifted the point labels by one to the right

{\hyperlink{eqref:TQHHHtildeDef}{\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptI}}}}=\mathsf{J}^{00}_{34}\,\mathsf{J}^{00}_{35}\,\mathsf{y}_{45}\,,\qquad{\hyperlink{eqref:TQHHHtildeDef}{\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{2}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptI}}}}=\mathsf{J}^{00}_{34}\,\mathsf{J}^{00}_{45}\,\mathsf{y}_{35}\,,\qquad{\hyperlink{eqref:TQHHHtildeDef}{\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{3}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptI}}}}=\mathsf{J}^{00}_{35}\,\mathsf{J}^{00}_{45}\,\mathsf{y}_{34}\,.

(7.12)

Calling
\mathbb{E}_{i}^{(2,0,2)}
the resulting five-point structures, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{1}^{(2,0,2)}\equiv t_{(2,0,2)}^{\mathsf{OPE}}{\hyperlink{eqref:TQHHHtildeDef}{\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptI}}}}

\displaystyle=10\mathcal{A}_{123,45}+20\mathcal{A}_{145,23}+20\mathcal{A}_{245,13}+2\mathcal{A}_{345,12}-5\mathcal{A}_{12345}

(7.13)

\displaystyle-5\mathcal{A}_{12354}-5\mathcal{A}_{12453}-5\mathcal{A}_{12543}-20\mathcal{A}_{13245}-20\mathcal{A}_{13254}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{2}^{(2,0,2)}\equiv t_{(2,0,2)}^{\mathsf{OPE}}{\hyperlink{eqref:TQHHHtildeDef}{\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{2}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptI}}}}

\displaystyle=-10\mathcal{A}_{124,35}-20\mathcal{A}_{135,24}-20\mathcal{A}_{235,14}-2\mathcal{A}_{345,12}+5\mathcal{A}_{12354}

\displaystyle+5\mathcal{A}_{12435}+5\mathcal{A}_{12453}+5\mathcal{A}_{12534}+20\mathcal{A}_{13524}+20\mathcal{A}_{14235}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{3}^{(2,0,2)}\equiv t_{(2,0,2)}^{\mathsf{OPE}}{\hyperlink{eqref:TQHHHtildeDef}{\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{3}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptI}}}}

\displaystyle=10\mathcal{A}_{125,34}+20\mathcal{A}_{134,25}+20\mathcal{A}_{234,15}+2\mathcal{A}_{345,12}-5\mathcal{A}_{12345}

\displaystyle-5\mathcal{A}_{12435}-5\mathcal{A}_{12534}-5\mathcal{A}_{12543}-20\mathcal{A}_{13425}-20\mathcal{A}_{14325}\,.

Similar computations can be done for the other representations. Once we have all of them, we can expand the free correlator as follows

\mathcal{G}^{(5)}_{\mathrm{free}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2q+p\leq 4,\\
p\,\,\mathrm{even}\end{subarray}}\sum_{k}g_{k}^{(q,p,q)}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{5})\,\mathbb{E}_{k}^{(q,p,q)}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{5})\,.

(7.14)

7.3
Remarks for finite
\boldsymbol{N}

Before showing the computation for
\langle\mathcal{O}_{02}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle
at large
N
, let us make a few remarks about the free theory at finite
N
. At zero coupling, the operator
\mathcal{O}_{02}
is not the only one appearing in the leading OPE singularity: there is also the
Q^{2}{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}{}^{2}
descendant of the free Konishi
K=\tr\hskip 1.0pt\varphi^{2}
.
35
35

35

Note that this problem would also arise in perturbation theory at weak coupling, but not at large
N
because the Konishi operator becomes heavy in the supergravity approximation.
This superdescendant, in terms of free fields, is precisely the pure single trace contraction that we ignored when constructing the operator
\mathcal{O}_{02}
in subsection
4.1
. Among the operators exchanged in the
\mathcal{O}_{2}\times\mathcal{O}_{2}
OPE there are no other supermultiplets that can have a dimension-four scalar transforming in the
(2,0,2)
.

It follows that we should expect to find some (numerical) coefficients
\alpha_{1}
,
\alpha_{2}
and
\alpha_{3}
such that the following limit holds
36
36

36

Since
\Delta_{L}=4
the power of
x_{12}^{2}
vanishes.

\displaystyle\lim_{x_{1}\to x_{2}}\;\sum_{k=1}^{3}\alpha_{k}\hskip 1.0pt{\hyperlink{eqref:TQHHHtildeDef}{\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptI}}}}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{5})\hskip 1.0ptg_{i}^{(2,0,2)}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{5})=

(7.15)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{02}}\,\langle\mathcal{O}_{02}(x_{2},{\mathbf{S}}_{2})\mathcal{O}_{2}(x_{3},y_{3})\mathcal{O}_{2}(x_{4},y_{4})\mathcal{O}_{2}(x_{5},y_{5})\rangle

\displaystyle+\lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}(Q^{2}{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}{}^{2}\hskip-0.8ptK)}\,\langle(Q^{2}{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}{}^{2}\hskip-0.8ptK)(x_{2},{\mathbf{S}}_{2})\mathcal{O}_{2}(x_{3},y_{3})\mathcal{O}_{2}(x_{4},y_{4})\mathcal{O}_{2}(x_{5},y_{5})\rangle\,,

where the four-point functions on the right hand side are computed by Wick contractions. The first one is given explicitly in (
4.7
). The OPE coefficient of
\mathcal{O}_{02}
is known from
Dolan:2004iy
and the one of the Konishi can also be inferred from the
\hat{A}_{00,10}
of
Dolan:2004iy
, although we recomputed it by using
\mathcal{O}_{02}^{(\mathrm{st,1})}
of subsection
4.1
for self-consistency. They read
37
37

37

The first differs from
Dolan:2004iy
by a factor of
3/4
due to the normalization of the four-point tensor structures of
\langle\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle
. The latter was instead recomputed by Wick contractions directly.

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{02}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{6}}\left(1-\frac{3}{N^{2}-1}\right)^{1/2}\,,

(7.16a)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}(Q^{2}{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}{}^{2}\hskip-0.8ptK)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{3}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{2}-1}}\,,

(7.16b)

A simple computation shows that

\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{3}=\frac{5}{4}\,.

(7.17)

The specific value of these coefficients depends on how we normalize the tensor structures
\mathbb{E}_{i}^{(q,p,q)}
, therefore it is not meaningful. The important thing is that they do not depend on
x_{ij}
nor on
N
.

Another possible pitfall, that we report just as a side note, has to do with the
(0,4,0)
scalar. It contributes to the OPE via the following differential operator

t_{(0,4,0)}^{\mathsf{OPE}}(y_{1},y_{2},\partial_{y_{0}})=\frac{1}{2^{7}3^{2}}\mathsf{y}_{1\partial_{0}}^{2}\,\mathsf{y}_{2\partial_{0}}^{2}\,,

(7.18)

Unlike the previous case, there are no superdescendants that can contribute to this representation at dimension four. Thus the exchanged operator must be the superprimary of a half-BPS multiplet transforming in the
(0,4,0)
. However, there are two distinct such operators: one is an admixture of a single trace and a double trace operator and the other is a pure double trace

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\sqrt{N^{2}+1}}{\sqrt{(N^{2}-9)(N^{2}-4)(N^{2}-1)}}\,\left(\tr\hskip 1.0pt(\varphi\cdot y)^{4}-\frac{2N^{2}-3}{N(N^{2}+1)}\big(\tr\hskip 1.0pt(\varphi\cdot y)^{2}\big)^{2}\right)\,,

(7.19a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{4}^{(\mathrm{dt})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{N^{4}-1}}\,\big(\tr\hskip 1.0pt(\varphi\cdot y)^{2}\big)^{2}\,.

(7.19b)

The relative coefficient in (
7.19a
) is obtained by requiring that the two combinations are orthogonal
Aprile:2018efk
;
Aprile:2020uxk
and the overall coefficients simply normalize the two-point functions to one. As argued in
Aprile:2018efk
, the three-point function
\langle\mathcal{O}_{4}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle
should vanish. On the other hand, from the conformal block expansion in
Dolan:2004iy
we see a nonzero OPE coefficient, there called
C_{2}
. By doing Wick contractions explicitly we see that the operator with vanishing OPE coefficient is
\mathcal{O}_{4}
and the one with coefficient
C_{2}
is
\mathcal{O}_{4}^{(\mathrm{dt})}
. Therefore we conclude that the operator exchanged in the
\mathcal{O}_{2}\times\mathcal{O}_{2}
OPE is the pure double trace one. If we match the OPE limit as explained earlier we again find simple numerical coefficients
\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{3}=60
.

7.4
OPE limit of the supergravity result

Now we are ready to take the OPE
x_{1}\to x_{2}
limit of the supergravity result of
Goncalves:2019znr
. From the representation in (
7.5
) it is easy to take the limit at coincident points. Indeed, when the right hand side converges, we have

\lim_{x_{1}\to x_{2}}D_{\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}\cdots\Delta_{n}}(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n})=D_{\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}\,\Delta_{3}\cdots\Delta_{n}}(x_{2},\ldots,x_{n})\,.

(7.20)

When the external dimensions are integers one can often compute the
D
-functions by using some differential recursion relations. For example, the
D
-functions appearing in the supergravity result of the five-point function can all be obtained by applying the following recursion relations

D_{\Delta_{1}\cdots\Delta_{i}+1\cdots\Delta_{j}+1\cdots\Delta_{n}}=\frac{2-\Sigma}{\Delta_{i}\Delta_{j}}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{ij}^{2}}D_{\Delta_{1}\cdots\Delta_{n}}\,,

(7.21)

on the seed function
D_{11112}
, where we defined
{\Sigma\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}+\Delta_{3}+\Delta_{4})}
. The integral
D_{11112}
is a one-loop pentagon diagram, known from
Bern:1993kr
;
Bern:1992em
. The same strategy works also for four points. In that case, however, the seed function is taken to be
D_{1111}
, which is divergent. This obstacle is overcome by removing a divergent prefactor and applying the recursion relations on the reduced function
{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{1111}
, defined in appendix
F
.

Now we project onto the representation
(2,0,2)
using the tensors
\mathbb{E}_{k}^{(2,0,2)}
defined in (
7.13
) and we add up the first two labels of the
D
-functions appearing in (
7.4
). It is easy to see that most functions obtained this way can be written as derivatives acting on
{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{1111}
, using the relations in (
7.21
). In the
(2,0,2)
sector there are only two exceptions up to permutations:

D_{3111}(z,{\bar{z}})\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad D_{4112}(z,{\bar{z}})\,.

(7.22)

These functions are indeed divergent. We can see this using the representation found in
Dolan:2004iy

{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}}(z,{\bar{z}})=\frac{\Gamma(\Delta_{1}-s)\Gamma(\Delta_{2}-s)}{(z{\bar{z}})^{s}}\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muE\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}}(z,{\bar{z}})+{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}}(z,{\bar{z}})_{\mathrm{reg.}}\,,

(7.23)

where we introduced

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}-\Delta_{3}-\Delta_{4})\,,\qquad s\in\mathbb{N}\,,

(7.24)

\displaystyle\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muE\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma(\Delta_{3})\Gamma(\Delta_{4})}{\Gamma(\Delta_{3}+\Delta_{4})}\sum_{m=0}^{s-1}(-1)^{m}(s-m-1)!\frac{(\Delta_{1}-s)_{m}(\Delta_{2}-s)_{m}(\Delta_{3})_{m}(\Delta_{4})_{m}}{m!(\Delta_{3}+\Delta_{4})_{2m}}

\displaystyle\times\,u^{m}\,{}_{2}F_{1}(\Delta_{2}-s+m,\Delta_{3}+m;\Delta_{3}+\Delta_{4}+2m;z+{\bar{z}}-z{\bar{z}})\,,

and
{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}}(z,{\bar{z}})_{\mathrm{reg.}}
is finite. In order to regularize the divergent
D
-functions we can shift all dimensions by
\epsilon
. The
\Gamma(\Delta_{2}-s)
function at the numerator is responsible for the divergence. If we denote for convenience
n_{\epsilon}\equiv n+\epsilon
, the regularized
{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}
functions read

\displaystyle{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{3_{\epsilon}\,1_{\epsilon}\,1_{\epsilon}\,1_{\epsilon}}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{u\hskip 1.0pt\epsilon}-\frac{2\gamma_{E}}{u}+\frac{v\log v}{u\hskip 1.0pt(1-v)}+{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{3111}(z,{\bar{z}})_{\mathrm{reg.}}\,,

(7.25)

\displaystyle{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{4_{\epsilon}\,1_{\epsilon}\,1_{\epsilon}\,2_{\epsilon}}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{u\hskip 1.0pt\epsilon}-\frac{2\gamma_{E}}{u}-\frac{v(1-v+v\log v)}{u\hskip 1.0pt(1-v)^{2}}+{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{4112}(z,{\bar{z}})_{\mathrm{reg.}}\,,

\displaystyle{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{4_{\epsilon}\,1_{\epsilon}\,2_{\epsilon}\,1_{\epsilon}}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{u\hskip 1.0pt\epsilon}-\frac{2\gamma_{E}}{u}+\frac{1}{u\hskip 1.0pt(1-v)}-\frac{v\left(v-2\right)\log v}{u(1-v)^{2}}+{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{4121}(z,{\bar{z}})_{\mathrm{reg.}}\,,

\displaystyle{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{4_{\epsilon}\,2_{\epsilon}\,1_{\epsilon}\,1_{\epsilon}}(z,{\bar{z}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{u^{2}\hskip 1.0pt\epsilon}-\frac{2\gamma_{E}}{u^{2}}-\frac{1+v}{u\hskip 1.0pt(1-v)^{2}}+\frac{v\left((1-v)^{2}-2u\right)\log v}{u^{2}(1-v)^{3}}+{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{4211}(z,{\bar{z}})_{\mathrm{reg.}}\,.

Notice that, unlike the regular part, the singular part of
{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{4121}
does not satisfy the identities in (
F.4
) and to compute it we have to resort to the explicit formulas in
Dolan:2004iy
. Quite nicely, the divergences cancel in the end result because the above
D
-functions appear only through the following combination

a\,{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{4112}+b\,{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{4121}+c\hskip 1.0ptu\,{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{4211}+d\,{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{3111}\sim\frac{a+b+c+d}{u\hskip 1.0pt\epsilon}+O(1)\,,

(7.26)

with
a+b+c+d=0
.
38
38

38

As a side remark, one could do the same computation for the
(0,4,0)
and in that case the divergences cancel in the same way but
{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{3111}
never appears, namely we have
d=a+b+c=0
.
,
39
39

39

Note that the cancellation of these divergences can be seen manifestly by using the representation of the five-point Witten diagram in terms of box integrals
Bern:1993kr
. We thank Xinan Zhou for point this out to us.
For the regular part of these
{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}
-functions we find

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{3111}(z,{\bar{z}})_{\mathrm{reg.}}&=R_{1}(z,{\bar{z}})\left(2\text{Li}_{2}(z)-2\text{Li}_{2}({\bar{z}})+\log\left(\frac{1-z}{1-{\bar{z}}}\right)\log(z{\bar{z}})\right)+\\
&\quad\;R_{2}(z,{\bar{z}})\log(z{\bar{z}})+R_{3}(z,{\bar{z}})\log\left((1-z)(1-{\bar{z}})\right)\,,\\
R_{1}(z,{\bar{z}})&=\frac{2(z-1)({\bar{z}}-1)}{(z-{\bar{z}})^{3}}\,,\\
R_{2}(z,{\bar{z}})&=-\frac{z+{\bar{z}}-2}{(z-{\bar{z}})^{2}}\,,\\
R_{3}(z,{\bar{z}})&=\frac{(z-1)({\bar{z}}-1)}{z{\bar{z}}}\left(\frac{z+{\bar{z}}}{(z-{\bar{z}})^{2}}-\frac{1}{z+{\bar{z}}-z{\bar{z}}}\right)\,,\end{aligned}

(7.27a)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{4112}(z,{\bar{z}})_{\mathrm{reg.}}&=\tilde{R}_{1}(z,{\bar{z}})\left(2\text{Li}_{2}(z)-2\text{Li}_{2}({\bar{z}})+\log\left(\frac{1-z}{1-{\bar{z}}}\right)\log(z{\bar{z}})\right)+\\
&\quad\;\tilde{R}_{2}(z,{\bar{z}})\log(z{\bar{z}})+\tilde{R}_{3}(z,{\bar{z}})\log\left((1-z)(1-{\bar{z}})\right)+\tilde{R}_{4}(z,{\bar{z}})\,,\\
\tilde{R}_{1}(z,{\bar{z}})&=-\frac{6(z-1)^{2}({\bar{z}}-1)^{2}(z+{\bar{z}})}{(z-{\bar{z}})^{5}}\,,\\
\tilde{R}_{2}(z,{\bar{z}})&=\frac{1}{(z-{\bar{z}})^{4}}\left(z\left(z^{2}({\bar{z}}-2)+z\left(5{\bar{z}}^{2}-16{\bar{z}}+9\right)+9{\bar{z}}-6\right)+z\leftrightarrow{\bar{z}}\right)\,,\\
\tilde{R}_{3}(z,{\bar{z}})&=\frac{(z-1)^{2}({\bar{z}}-1)^{2}}{z{\bar{z}}}\left(\frac{1}{(z+{\bar{z}}-z{\bar{z}})^{2}}-\frac{z^{2}+10z{\bar{z}}+{\bar{z}}^{2}}{(z-{\bar{z}})^{4}}\right)\,,\\
\tilde{R}_{4}(z,{\bar{z}})&=\frac{(z-1)({\bar{z}}-1)(z+{\bar{z}}-4)}{(z-{\bar{z}})^{2}(z+{\bar{z}}-z{\bar{z}})}\,.\end{aligned}

(7.27b)

The regular part of
{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{4121}(z,{\bar{z}})_{\mathrm{reg.}}
and
{\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}_{4211}(z,{\bar{z}})_{\mathrm{reg.}}
can be obtained from the identities in (
F.4
).

After having regularized all the
D
-function we can finally take the OPE limit and explicitly see that the free theory result is perfectly matched at order
1/N^{3}
and the non-rational pieces in the
D
-functions all cancel each other. This observation supports conjectured claims that the three-point functions of all the exchanged short multiplets are protected
Goncalves:2019znr
;
Heslop:2003xu
. The very same situation occurs when we select
\mathcal{O}_{4}^{(\mathrm{dt})}
in the OPE of
\mathcal{O}_{2}\times\mathcal{O}_{2}
, namely the supergravity result perfectly reproduces
\langle\mathcal{O}_{4}^{(\mathrm{dt})}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{2}\rangle^{\mathrm{free}}
up to order
1/N^{3}
.

Section 8
Outlook

In this paper we have revived the study of quarter-BPS operators in the context of four dimensional
\mathcal{N}=4
SYM. In particular we have found constraints imposed by the underlying chiral algebra on the structure of the protected part of the four-point correlators, involving one or more quarter-BPS operators. There are several directions that would be interesting to pursue. We list some below.

Superconformal blocks

: in order to fully exploit the power of superconformal symmetry, it would be very useful and interesting to find the form of the superconformal blocks. Differently from the half-BPS case
Dolan:2001tt
, they are not known. Being them eigenfunctions of the quartic and quadratic Casimir of the superconformal group, it could be possible to explore the superspace approach to solve the eigenvalue problem.

Numerical bootstrap

: it would be nice to explore the mixed correlator system using numerical bootstrap techniques. Despite the fact that we do not use the full superconformal symmetry, it can still be possible to obtain information on the dimensions and OPE coefficients of the intermediate non protected operators. In particular, this method can be helpful to see if there are some OPE coefficients which are protected.

Basis of function

: it would be interesting to understand if the basis of
\bar{D}
functions is enough to compute the correlators involving double trace operators. It has been observed, in a holographic computation, that such a basis is not enough and needs to be supplemented with more general functions
Ceplak:2021wzz
. We hope to be able to clarify this point in the future.

Higher point functions

: in this paper we have made a comparison with a five-point correlator computation
Goncalves:2019znr
. It would be very interesting to be able to use our results together with supersymmetry constraints and more generally the results of
Antunes:2021kmm
, to find some structures of the six-point correlators of half-BPS operators.

Triple trace operators

: in the context of holographic computations to get the corrections in the large central charge limit of the four-point correlators of
\mathcal{O}_{2}
as in
Bissi:2020woe
;
Bissi:2020wtv
, the anomalous dimensions and the OPE coefficients of triple trace operators are needed. It would be interesting to use the results of this paper for the leading twist, non protected, triple trace operator appearing in the OPE of
\mathcal{O}_{2}\times\mathcal{O}_{02}
and study the constraints imposed by crossing symmetry given by its presence, at the level of the four-point function.
